# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, SQLite in the production CLI, one platform binary at a time. A separately enabled Postgres evaluation adapter is not a hosted/deployable target (Moves below).

## Moves

- **2026-10-13 — stored lower lifetimes (`not before`) are commissioned by PAGE2** (LLP 3000.036.003). This unblocks timed disclosure without an application publication job. Per-application jobs that flip a publication flag leave this admitted path; clock expressions in rules, captured lower lifetimes for read-through children, and boundary recounts for maintained data remain excluded. HS owns handshake transport; PAGE2 declares the new semantic capability.

- **2026-10-06 — exact Following history (FH delegated round-1 ruling).**
  Admit a Following-specific server traversal and bounded volatile frontier/replay
  state under LLP 3000.026 §9. The approved design reads exact history online
  from the first page; the SDK will own continuation. The first milestone is internal event metadata
  and the oracle, with no public completeness claim. Taken off: a blanket ban on
  state for this one expiring read. General result caches, resumable queries,
  device traversal, inbox coverage certificates and history-only blocks remain
  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.

- **2026-09-26 — server operation latency (LLP 3000.019 §3.7).** Calibration #22
  measured each read once and could not claim repeated p95/degradation testing.
  Unblocks percentile measurements of the application's own load without a
  custom timing harness. Taken off: a latency harness written by the builder;
  general metrics, tracing infrastructure and an admin console remain excluded.

- **2026-09-23 — Quarry states intent moves onto the doing-list** (Charlie:
  "if you need to try making *significant* architectural reworks in order to
  make the data modeling, queries, and scalability be excellent and elegant,
  you can experiment with that"; LLP 3000.013). Unblocks reads that state
  predicates and order, one `view` declaration for derived data, Following
  without caps, jobs and aggregates without author-chosen bounds, an app
  object and an offline web shell. Taken off: index names and author-chosen
  engine numbers on the paved path; the timeline's promotion, pull routes and
  caps; the "Time in rules" refusal is unchanged. Stands: one interpreter, no
  JavaScript in the data plane, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no relevance-ranked
  search.

- **2026-09-16 — the remaining general-purpose program moves onto the doing-list**
  (Charlie: "complete everything ... we'll remove services when we are done
  with the workroom test rather than wait to add them in ... use the fleet";
  LLP 3000.009 §14.7). Unblocks the §7 integrated-service baseline, C3 production
  Postgres, D's authorized database capabilities and measurement-led C2/E
  work while Workroom continues. Taken off: waiting for Workroom before
  starting services, and keeping production Postgres integration outside the
  current implementation program. The completed Workroom record drives a
  retain/remove pass. Stands: one interpreter and managed writer, no managed
  SQL writes, no JavaScript transactions, no SQL in the first Workroom cell,
  no invented performance/real-provider evidence, no expo-build-1000 load,
  and no new publication or managed-service deployment authority.

- **2026-09-16 — application SQL reads move onto the doing-list** (Charlie: "admit application SQL reads"; LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4, increment D). Unblocks reporting and search over authorized projection inputs and declared database-owned tables, with authorization before selection, ordering and limits. Taken off in the same change: the inspection-only exclusion for application SQL and D's pending-author gate. Implementation and qualification remain pending; SQLite and Postgres halves ship together, channels follow candidates, and A1–A3 plus the fixture pack stay first. Stands: no SQL writes into managed data, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no SQL in the first Snapback brief cell, and no publication authorization.

- **2026-09-15 — a Postgres evaluation backend moves onto the doing-list** (author: "Build the evaluation prototype"). Unblocks concrete evaluation of storage agnosticism, interpreter/database round trips and transactional sync ordering. This bounded prototype replaces full CLI/deployment integration as the next backend task; those product surfaces remain off this increment's doing-list. It shares Quarry, rules, maintains and partition logic, but uses an experimental Postgres store. Native SQL exists only as an explicitly separate comparison workload, not an application escape hatch. SQLite remains the production and local-device store. See [the experiment](./experiments/postgres/README.md).

- **2026-09-15 — the server half of LLP 3000.009 moves onto the doing-list** (author: "begin implementation using astra xhigh to code"; LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increments A1–A3). Unblocks the online-only brief's invitation, webhook and scheduled journeys without a second application server: work records whose authority is bound at admission, ids bound to their operation and arguments, declared action results, a generated handler context with a durable record per external step, declared routes and webhooks, scheduled actions under service seats, and `serve` supervising the runtime. Taken off in the same change: "A jobs framework" narrows — actions, effects and scheduled actions get per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation; priorities, worker pools and dead letters stay out — and the actions card's "supervise the worker as an ordinary Node process" leaves. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no identity provider in the engine, and no `ctx.sql`, projections or SQL reads in this increment.

- **2026-09-15 — the Postgres access boundary moves onto the doing-list** (LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increment C1). Unblocks measuring Postgres behind a batched access boundary instead of row-at-a-time calls: transaction-scoped prefetch, a pipelined commit tail, one portable index-key bound checked on both stores, and the corpus, conformance and device suites on both stores. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation prototype's single-adapter framing, in which the only next step was an unbatched store. Stands: Postgres is not a hosted or deployable target; its refusals of assets, jobs, actions and schedules, and `serve`, `deploy` and backup on Postgres, wait for increment C3.

- **2026-09-13 — word search, effects, service seats and presence move onto the doing-list** (author: "add all the things we need for Clique and Interview"; the full-brief Clique record's remaining capability lines and Interview's adapter responsibilities). What it unblocks: Clique M5's three search scopes online and offline on one index, M6's push seam without connection leases hand-built in tables, M8's digest emitted rather than logged into a table, Interview's phone sign-in and model workers as a service that mints sessions instead of proxying every request, and the ranked feed of LLP 3000.004 on the `feeds` card. Taken off in the same change: "an embedded JS engine for effects, until measured" (the engine never calls out; a worker does), "word search" from the package's exclusions. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no relevance ranking, no identity provider in the engine.
- **2026-09-12 — expiry, assets and jobs move onto the doing-list** (author: "complete snapback4 to the point where the entire clique benchmark can be run"). What it unblocks: the struck half of the Clique brief (M4 media, M5 view-once and expiry with no client open, M8's scheduled digest). Taken off in the same change: the "one photo path" and "cron syntax" exclusions above narrow to transcoding and to cron beyond `every N [at HH:MM]`; the "time in rules" exclusion stands, answered by `expire at .col` rather than a rule.
- **2026-09-13 — native jobs on (author: "write up an LLP about this and then implement").** Unblocks the scoring half of a feed — floats, vectors, real loops, a typed schema — without putting any of it into Quarry, and gives the model half of LLP 3000.004 a seat inside the bounds: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings, fuel, no I/O. Off the doing-list in the same change: the "external ranker with a service seat" of LLP 3000.004 §6.5 is no longer the only shape for compute over the store; it remains the shape for the network. Ranked and vector search, effects execution and an embedded JS engine stay above.
- **2026-09-13 — `sql`, a fifth named observability verb, moves onto the doing-list** (author ruling on LLP 3000.007 §14.10). Read-only SQL over generated per-table views of the developer's own store, under `dev` or against a `--release` directory; never rule-filtered, never an application surface. What it unblocks: the ground truth two Clique essays said Snapback lacked beside Postgres ("there is no underlying Postgres to query"), and the inspection the 0.2.6 Astra lane did by copying its SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: SQL reads as an application query surface (LLP 3000.007 §9.1) leave the doing-list until the reporting gap is measured after the language relaxations; reporting is Quarry's, inspection is `sql`'s. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, one authoring language.

- **2026-09-14 — the simulator witness is published for app builders (LLP 3000.007 §20.10, S30).** Unblocks native offline proof artifacts; replaces the repository-only Metro witness on the doing-list. Real-browser and device witnesses still cover only what the deterministic simulator cannot reach.

- **2026-09-14 — development clock control (S27, calibration F5)** moves onto the doing-list to unblock expiry and scheduled-digest tests without real-minute waits or backdated seeds; waiting on real time leaves the development test path, while production clock controls stay off the doing-list.

- **2026-09-25 — ephemeral tables enact the standing realtime line of LLP 3000.009 §12 (LLP 3000.017).** Unblocks typing and viewing without durable outbox traffic. Nothing leaves the doing-list: this enacts an already admitted scope. Last-seen storage, multi-server presence, a separate presence stream and ephemeral authority over durable invariants remain excluded.

- **2026-09-30 — bounded client subscription inspection (author's PR round-0 task)**
  moves onto the doing-list: query-group queue/in-flight/withdrawal counts and
  bounded latency/cause samples explain mounted screens flashing empty. Taken off
  the doing-list: builders must reconstruct this pressure from ad-hoc console
  instrumentation. General observability beyond the named verbs remains excluded;
  there is no telemetry transport or server admin console (LLP 3000.023 §10).
memory isolation. Fixture and browser proofs now explicitly request memory;
fixture tools also opt into unsafe doors. Packed qualification runs the published
fixture, client developmentHosts and browser fences verbatim and checks that
fixture hosts preserve a prepopulated persistent developer database.

### STAGE: complete guide corpus integration (2026-10-18)

The full language guide suite additionally required the staging diagnostic
family in why/definition/recovery registries, exact application-file and mutation
census entries, keyword-list integration, compact card openings and a cursor
on the staging note list. These are now reconciled without raising budgets
or weakening corpus checks. The updated guide schema and opening host fence
are exercised from the final installed pack.
## 2026-10-18 — SEC: non-whitespace text minimums and security intent

The commissioned bakeoff security lane adds independent policy intent,
exposure analysis and generated challenges under LLP 3000.036.004. Core design
revision 3 and the operation/row refinement revision 5 each received READY
from Muse and a fresh Codex session; verbatim reviews/provenance are retained.
Text minimums now count non-whitespace Unicode scalars through the shared
argument/row validator. Maxima count all scalars and stored bytes are unchanged.
The two focused minimum tests pass, including optional/list arguments. Locked
Linux wasm is 2,372,893 B raw / 723,411 B gzip (+136 B gzip versus 0.4.31);
budget 723,911 B is measurement + exactly 500 B. Compiler security analysis
adds no device protocol. Qualification continues; no zero-hole claim is made.
STAGE owns first-admin/staging setup; RULES5 owns live-parent authority.


## 2026-10-18 — SEC: independent intent, exposure and generated challenges

LLP 3000.036.004 adds independent schema invariants, bounded exhaustive
three-valued implication, separate Lean lemmas, `audit`/`check --audit`,
and schema/fixture engine challenges in default `test`. Both independent
design revisions and implementation round 5 are READY from Muse and fresh
Codex. The checker preserves explicit unproved exposure paths and finite
counterexamples never become reachability proofs. The exposure map includes
inherited grants and their writers; new advice covers role/grant lifetimes,
grant arguments/global bootstrap, omitted evidence, sweep timing and refusal
oracles. The guide and all shipped todo/social/chat/production starter schemas
state their privacy intent. One new online-only guide mutation updates the
authoring census; the 94 private eligible sites stay unchanged.

Reports and all 150 schema hashes: `reports/20261018-sec/`. Main 20k and device
0–999 differentials have no disagreements; Lean's four known signature classes
retain the exact integration15 counts. Whole-protocol/device freshness,
general aggregate/time reductions, actual unauthenticated/revoked-device
campaigns and the repaired 015 custody workflow remain explicit gaps in
`../issues/20261018-sb4-security-proof-and-adversarial-qualification-gaps.md`.
This is a conservative verification foundation, not a zero-holes capability
claim. No version bump, push or publication.

use snapback4_lang::{compile, Bound, client_contracts::compare, emit};
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
fn checked(source: &str) -> Bound {
    let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);b
}
#[test]
fn literal_defaults_aliases_and_deprecated_inputs_execute_the_shared_ir() {
    let b=checked("query current(n: int 0..10 default 4, old: text <=8 deprecated):\n  require n != 9 else NINE\n  return n\nalias original = current\n");
    let mut engine=Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap();
    for name in ["current","original"] {
        for args in [BTreeMap::new(),BTreeMap::from([("n".into(),Value::Null)]),BTreeMap::from([("old".into(),Value::String("legacy".into()))])] {
            assert_eq!(engine.query(name,Context::new("user:a",args)).unwrap().data,Value::Int(4));
        }
        assert_eq!(engine.query(name,Context::new("user:a",BTreeMap::from([("n".into(),Value::Int(6))]))).unwrap().data,Value::Int(6));
        assert!(engine.query(name,Context::new("user:a",BTreeMap::from([("n".into(),Value::Int(11))]))).is_err());
        assert!(engine.query(name,Context::new("user:a",BTreeMap::from([("n".into(),Value::Int(9))]))).is_err());
        assert!(engine.query(name,Context::new("user:a",BTreeMap::from([("old".into(),Value::Int(2))]))).is_err());
    }
}
#[test]
fn invalid_modifiers_and_aliases_refuse_without_silent_semantic_changes() {
    for source in [
        "query q(n: int default 11):\n  return n\n".replace("int default", "int 0..10 default"),
        "query q(n: int default now):\n  return n\n".into(),
        "query q(n: int default null):\n  return n\n".into(),
        "query q(n: int default 1 deprecated):\n  return 1\n".into(),
        "query q(n: int deprecated):\n  return n\n".into(),
        "fn f(n: int default 1):\n  return n\nquery q():\n  return f(1)\n".into(),
        "job j(n: int default 1): native\n".into(),
        "query q():\n  return 1\nalias a = q\nalias b = a\n".into(),
        "query q():\n  return 1\nalias q = q\n".into(),
        "alias a = b\nalias b = a\n".into(),
    ] {assert!(!compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.clone())]).diagnostics.is_empty(),"{source}");}
}
#[test]
fn reports_old_input_breaks_results_and_refusals_and_keeps_required_types() {
    let old=checked("query removed():\n  return true\nquery q(n: int 0..10, obsolete: text <=20):\n  require n != 0 else ZERO\n  return {n}\n");
    let new=checked("query q(n: int 1..9, added: bool):\n  require n != 1 else ONE\n  return {value: n}\n");
    let report=compare(&old,&new,"release 1").join("\n");
    for text in ["removed operation","new required argument added","removed argument obsolete","argument n retyped or narrowed","RESULT REVIEW","BEHAVIOR REVIEW","release 1"] {assert!(report.contains(text),"{text}: {report}");}
    let api=emit::typescript_spanning(&new,&old,2);
    assert!(api.contains("*/ added: boolean"),"{api}");assert!(!api.contains("added?:"));
    let safe=checked("query current(n: int 0..20, obsolete: text <=20 deprecated, added: bool default false):\n  require n != 0 else ZERO\n  return {n}\nalias q = current\nalias removed = constant\nquery constant():\n  return true\n");
    assert!(compare(&old,&safe,"release 1").is_empty(),"{:?}",compare(&old,&safe,"release 1"));
}
#[test]
fn same_named_nominal_rows_still_report_changed_result_fields() {
    let old=checked("table items:\n  n: int\n  public\nquery q(id: items):\n  return items[id]\n");
    let new=checked("table items:\n  n: int\n  label: text <=20 default ''\n  public\nquery q(id: items):\n  return items[id]\n");
    assert!(compare(&old,&new,"release 1").iter().any(|s|s.contains("RESULT REVIEW")));
}

#[test]
fn defaults_and_aliases_have_device_parity_after_backend_json_roundtrip() {
    use snapback4_device::{device::{Backend,Device},memory::MemoryStore};
    let b=checked("query q(n: int default -4, old: bool deprecated):\n  return n\nalias legacy = q\n");
    let backend=Backend{generation:1,schema:b.schema,programs:b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program).collect()};
    let encoded=serde_json::to_string(&backend).unwrap();
    let decoded:Backend=snapback4_core::json_codec::from_text(&encoded).unwrap();
    let mut device=Device::open_store(MemoryStore::new(),Some(decoded)).unwrap();
    for op in ["q","legacy"] {
        for args in [serde_json::json!({}),serde_json::json!({"n":null,"old":true})] {
            assert_eq!(device.query(op,"user:a",&args,0).unwrap().data,Value::Int(-4));
        }
        assert_eq!(device.query(op,"user:a",&serde_json::json!({"n":7}),0).unwrap().data,Value::Int(7));
        assert!(device.query(op,"user:a",&serde_json::json!({"n":"bad"}),0).is_err());
    }
}

#[test]
fn projected_results_keep_declared_integer_and_text_domains() {
    let source="table items:\n  n: int 0..10\n  label: text 1..20\n  unused: int 0..10\n  public\nquery q(id: items):\n  row = items[id]! else NOT_FOUND\n  return {n: row.n, label: row.label}\n";
    let old=checked(source);
    for (from,to,field) in [("int 0..10","int 0..20","items.n"),("text 1..20","text 0..40","items.label")] {
        let new=checked(&source.replacen(from,to,1));
        assert!(compare(&old,&new,"release 1").iter().any(|line|line.contains("RESULT REVIEW")&&line.contains(field)),"{:?}",compare(&old,&new,"release 1"));
    }
    let unused=checked(&source.replace("unused: int 0..10","unused: int 0..100"));
    assert!(compare(&old,&unused,"release 1").is_empty(),"{:?}",compare(&old,&unused,"release 1"));
}

#[test]
fn projected_pages_and_accumulated_lists_keep_declared_result_domains() {
    let table="table items:\n  n: int 0..10\n  label: text 1..20\n  unused: int 0..10\n  public\n";
    for body in ["query q():\n  return items first 10 by .id {n, label}\n", "query q():\n  out = []\n  for row in items first 10 by .id:\n    out = out + [{n: row.n, label: row.label}]\n  return out\n"] {
        let source=format!("{table}{body}");let old=checked(&source);
        for (from,to,field) in [("n: int 0..10","n: int 0..20","items.n"),("text 1..20","text 0..20","items.label")] {
            let new=checked(&source.replace(from,to));let report=compare(&old,&new,"release 1");
            assert!(report.iter().any(|line|line.contains("RESULT REVIEW")&&line.contains(field)),"{body}: {report:?}");
        }
        let unused=checked(&source.replace("unused: int 0..10","unused: int 0..100"));
        assert!(compare(&old,&unused,"release 1").is_empty(),"{body}");
    }
}

#[test]
fn writes_walks_and_aggregate_selectors_keep_result_domains() {
    let table="table items:\n  n: int 0..10\n  label: text 1..20\n  unused: int 0..10\n  parent: items ?\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  update <- allow\n";
    for body in [
        "mutation q(id: items):\n  row = update items[id] {label: 'x'}\n  return {n: row.n, label: row.label}\n",
        "query q(id: items):\n  chain = walk items[id] up .parent depth 8\n  return chain {n, label}\n",
        "query q():\n  page = items first 10 by .id\n  return {n: min(page, .n), label: min(page, .label)}\n",
    ] {
        let source=format!("{table}{body}");let old=checked(&source);
        for (from,to,field) in [("n: int 0..10","n: int 0..20","items.n"),("text 1..20","text 0..20","items.label")] {
            let new=checked(&source.replace(from,to));let report=compare(&old,&new,"release 1");
            assert!(report.iter().any(|line|line.contains("RESULT REVIEW")&&line.contains(field)),"{body}: {report:?}");
        }
        let unused=checked(&source.replace("unused: int 0..10","unused: int 0..100"));
        assert!(compare(&old,&unused,"release 1").is_empty(),"{body}: {:?}",compare(&old,&unused,"release 1"));
    }
}
#[test]
fn declared_action_results_keep_bounds_that_typescript_erases() {
    let source="use server\naction fetch() returns {n: int 0..10, label: text 1..20}\n";
    let old=checked(source);
    for changed in [source.replace("int 0..10","int 0..20"),source.replace("text 1..20","text 0..20")] {
        assert!(compare(&old,&checked(&changed),"release 1").iter().any(|line|line.contains("RESULT REVIEW")));
    }
}

#[test]
fn whole_rows_and_write_images_keep_declared_text_minima() {
    let table="table items:\n  n: int 0..10\n  label: text 1..20\n  public\n  update <- allow\n";
    for body in ["query q(id: items):\n  return items[id]\n", "query q():\n  return items first 10 by .id\n", "mutation q(id: items):\n  return update items[id] {n: 1}\n"] {
        let source=format!("{table}{body}");let old=checked(&source);let new=checked(&source.replace("text 1..20","text 0..20"));
        assert!(compare(&old,&new,"release 1").iter().any(|line|line.contains("RESULT REVIEW")),"{body}");
    }
}

#[test]
fn scalar_returning_writes_keep_their_payload_and_lookup_domains() {
    let table="table items:\n  n: int 0..10\n  label: text 1..20\n  unused: int ?\n  unique label\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  update <- allow\n";
    for body in ["mutation q(n: int, label: text <=20):\n  insert items {n, label}\n  return true\n", "mutation q(id: items, n: int, label: text <=20):\n  update items[id] {n, label}\n  return true\n", "mutation q(label: text <=20, n: int):\n  upsert items[label = label] {n}\n  return true\n"] {
        let source=format!("{table}{body}");let old=checked(&source);
        for (from,to,field) in [("n: int 0..10","n: int 0..20","items.n"),("text 1..20","text 0..20","items.label")] {
            let new=checked(&source.replace(from,to));assert!(compare(&old,&new,"release 1").iter().any(|line|line.contains("RESULT REVIEW")&&line.contains(field)),"{body}: {:?}",compare(&old,&new,"release 1"));
        }
        let unused=checked(&source.replace("unused: int ?","unused: int 0..100 ?"));
        assert!(compare(&old,&unused,"release 1").is_empty(),"{body}: {:?}",compare(&old,&unused,"release 1"));
    }
}

 ```
 ## Trigger a deployed job
 A running serve accepts `POST /jobs/<job>` with a full service token; the job executes as the system on its FIFO queue and server clock. Every full service seat can trigger system jobs, so provision these tokens only to trusted operators. Guest/account sessions, read-scoped tokens and personas cannot use this door. `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` remains development-only.
@@ -148,3 +148,49 @@ snapback4 deploy ./deployment --schema ./bridge --check
 ```
 
 The new versions/v1 captures selected modules, their contemporary fixtures and `versions/v1/release.json` with the actual immutable release id, source hash and CLI version. Unchanged deploy keeps its id; existing snapshots never change. Choose a new positive-numbered versions/vN; saving cannot combine with --check. Saved-source deploy uses its own fixtures. Use the recorded id when choosing a baseline; since_release remains the author-supplied newer-schema label. The bridge starts from complete current Quarry source and this app's `bridge/ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md`. Retain current domains, audiences, guards and quotas; repair records through authorized mutations; prove blocked cases refuse and affected lists/counts remain correct; repeat the old-source preview. No bridge is activated or business migration synthesized. JSON includes checklist; --verbose retains full evidence/index paths. Dormant rows reserve ids but no unique slots: new active rows may block restoration. A widened non-unique bridge permits repair before reinstating uniqueness; retained storage cannot supply absent safety logic.
+
+## Keep v1 clients working
+
+`deploy --check --json` reports `clientCompatibility` separately from data
+adoption: missing operations, changed kinds, required new arguments, removed or
+narrowed inputs, result shape/value changes and changed refusal codes/messages.
+Deploy prints the same warnings. Input breaks name the old release and the
+E_INPUT/E_OP outcome. A result or behavior review asks you to verify the intended
+contract; a correct new privacy denial is intentional behavior, not an input
+shape failure. The comparison is conservative, not a proof of unchanged logic.
+
+For example, save this v1 source and its callers sending
+`{name:"Ada",obsolete:"old input"}` to `greet`:
+
+```quarry
+use identity guests
+query greet(name: text <=40, obsolete: text <=40):
+  return {message: name + '!'}
+```
+
+Deploy this v2 source. The unchanged v1 call still returns `{message:"Ada!"}`;
+new callers can use `greeting` and omit `suffix` and `obsolete`:
+
+```quarry
+use identity guests
+query greeting(name: text <=40, suffix: text <=1 default '!', obsolete: text <=40 deprecated):
+  return {message: name + suffix}
+alias greet = greeting
+```
+
+A literal default handles omitted or null arguments and must fit the declared
+scalar type; explicit non-null input still validates. `deprecated` accepts a
+validated optional argument but forbids referring to it in the body. Unknown
+arguments still refuse. These modifiers apply to non-native Quarry queries and
+mutations; helpers, actions and jobs keep their distinct invocation contracts.
+Aliases target an authored query/mutation in the same module, carry the same
+rules/guards/results, and cannot chain. Retain the old endpoint until its callers
+have retired; preserve the old result projection and handle intended denials.
+
+Deploy also warns when later rollback can refuse or hide rows: dropping a
+required old column or widening a value domain can strand newly written rows.
+The preflight separately attempts exact reverse adoption on its post-deploy
+snapshot, naming present-data failures. Keep old fields and compatible domains
+in an expand/contract bridge; after writes run the named
+`rollback <deployment> --to N --check --bridge <new-dir>` before selecting N.
+A green snapshot never freezes later writes.

diff --git a/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/release-transitions.test.mjs b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/release-transitions.test.mjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..8c6f5cdcfa
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/release-transitions.test.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,11 @@
+import test from 'node:test';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import { cli, startServe } from '../dist/proof.js';
+import { releaseTransitions } from '../../../scripts/release-transitions-cases.mjs';
+test('release reactivation, refusal cleanup, rollback foresight and frozen v1 guide callers', {timeout:180_000}, async () => {
+  const original=process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN;
+  process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN??=fileURLToPath(new URL('../../../target/debug/snapback4',import.meta.url));
+  try {await releaseTransitions({cli,startServe},tmpdir());}
+  finally {if(original===undefined)delete process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN;else process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN=original;}
+});
diff --git a/snapback4/scripts/publication-release-transitions.mjs b/snapback4/scripts/publication-release-transitions.mjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..ad420a7b3a
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/scripts/publication-release-transitions.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,13 @@
+// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000#11-old-client-operation-contracts-rel5 — the installed guide is the source exercised by packed qualification.
+import {readFileSync, writeFileSync, rmSync} from 'node:fs';
+import {join} from 'node:path';
+import {runToolchainCommand} from './publication-starters.mjs';
+export async function proveReleaseTransitions(cwd) {
+  const path=join(cwd,'release-transitions.mjs');
+  const source=readFileSync(new URL('./release-transitions-cases.mjs',import.meta.url),'utf8');
+  writeFileSync(path,source+`\nimport {cli,startServe} from 'snapback4/proof';\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(await releaseTransitions({cli,startServe},process.cwd())));\n`);
+  const env={...process.env};delete env.SNAPBACK4_BIN;delete env.PORT_LO;delete env.PORT_HI;
+  try {await runToolchainCommand(process.execPath,[path],{cwd,env,timeout:240_000});}
+  finally {rmSync(path,{force:true});}
+  return {status:'passed',cases:['reactivation','refused-deploy-cleanliness','rollback-foresight','old-client-diff','installed-v1-v2-guide']};
+}

diff --git a/snapback4/scripts/release-transitions-cases.mjs b/snapback4/scripts/release-transitions-cases.mjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..b0bdfd4be7
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/scripts/release-transitions-cases.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,135 @@
+// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#16-release-reactivation-and-rollback-foresight-rel5 — run the same transitions with development and installed binaries.
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
+import { mkdirSync, writeFileSync, readFileSync, readdirSync, mkdtempSync, rmSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { join } from 'node:path';
+
+export async function releaseTransitions({ cli, startServe }, parent) {
+  const root=mkdtempSync(join(parent,'release-transitions-'));
+  const active=new Set();
+  try {
+    const fixture=name=> {
+      const cwd=join(root,name);mkdirSync(join(cwd,'snapback'),{recursive:true});
+      const deployment=join(cwd,'deployment'),schema=join(cwd,'snapback/schema.q');
+      const invoke=args=>cli(args,{cwd,timeoutMs:60_000});
+      const run=async args=>{const r=await invoke(args);assert.equal(r.code,0,r.stdout+r.stderr);return r;};
+      const status=async()=>JSON.parse((await run(['releases',deployment,'--json'])).stdout);
+      const releases=()=>readdirSync(join(deployment,'releases')).sort();
+      const source=text=>writeFileSync(schema,text);
+      const serve=async()=> {
+        const host=await startServe(deployment,{cwd,timeoutMs:60_000});active.add(host);
+        const signed=await (await fetch(host.url+'/auth/guest',{method:'POST',body:'{}'})).json();
+        assert.ok(signed.session?.token,JSON.stringify(signed));
+        const headers={'content-type':'application/json',authorization:'Bearer '+signed.session.token};
+        const post=async(path,args={},id)=> {
+          const r=await fetch(host.url+path,{method:'POST',headers,body:JSON.stringify({args,...(id?{id}:{})}),signal:AbortSignal.timeout(30_000)});
+          return r.json();
+        };
+        return {host,post};
+      };
+      return {cwd,deployment,schema,invoke,run,status,releases,source,serve};
+    };
+    const f=fixture('reactivation');
+    const v1=`use identity guests
+table items:
+  body: text <=40
+  public 'release proof'
+  insert <- allow
+  update <- allow
+mutation add(body: text <=40):
+  return insert items {body}
+query list():
+  return items first 20 by .id
+`;
+    const v2=v1.replace('  body: text','  extra: text <=40 default \'default\'\n  body: text')+`mutation mark(id: items):
+  return update items[id] {extra: 'preserved'}
+`;
+    f.source(v1);await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment]);
+    f.source(v2);await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment]);await f.run(['rollback',f.deployment,'--to','1']);
+    const before=await f.status();
+    const check=JSON.parse((await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout);
+    assert.equal(check.plan.release,2);assert.deepEqual(await f.status(),before);assert.deepEqual(f.releases(),['1','2']);
+    assert.equal(JSON.parse((await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment,'--json'])).stdout).release,2);
+    const {host,post}=await f.serve();
+    const added=await post('/m/add',{body:'kept'},'rel5-add');assert.equal(added.state,'sent',JSON.stringify(added));
+    const id=added.result.id;
+    assert.equal((await post('/m/mark',{id},'rel5-mark')).state,'sent');
+    await f.run(['rollback',f.deployment,'--to','1','--restore-dormant']);
+    await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment]);
+    assert.equal((await f.status()).release,2);assert.equal((await post('/q/list')).data[0].extra,'preserved');
+    const unchanged=await f.status();assert.equal(JSON.parse((await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment,'--json'])).stdout).unchanged,true);
+    assert.deepEqual(await f.status(),unchanged);
+    f.source(v2.replace('  body: text','  required: int\n  body: text').replace('insert items {body}','insert items {body, required: 1}'));
+    const failed=await f.invoke(['deploy',f.deployment]);assert.equal(failed.code,1);assert.match(failed.stderr,/E_ADOPT/);
+    assert.deepEqual(f.releases(),['1','2']);assert.deepEqual(await f.status(),unchanged);
+    assert.equal((await post('/q/list')).data[0].extra,'preserved');
+    await host.stop();active.delete(host);
+    // Failure after validation, before database commit, also cleans its numeric source.
+    f.source(v2.replace("return items first 20", "return items first 19"));
+    rmSync(join(f.deployment,'current'));mkdirSync(join(f.deployment,'current'));
+    const pointerFailure=await f.invoke(['deploy',f.deployment]);assert.equal(pointerFailure.code,1);assert.match(pointerFailure.stderr,/current must be a regular/);
+    assert.deepEqual(f.releases(),['1','2']);rmSync(join(f.deployment,'current'),{recursive:true});writeFileSync(join(f.deployment,'current'),'2\n');
+
+    const g=fixture('foresight');
+    const prior=`use identity guests
+table gauges:
+  minPressure: int 0..2000
+  public 'rollback proof'
+  insert <- allow
+mutation add():
+  return insert gauges {minPressure: 4}
+`;
+    const next=prior.replace('  minPressure: int 0..2000\n','  lowPressure: int 0..2000 default 0\n').replace('{minPressure: 4}','{lowPressure: 4}');
+    g.source(prior);await g.run(['deploy',g.deployment]);g.source(next);
+    const foresight=JSON.parse((await g.run(['deploy',g.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout);
+    assert.ok(foresight.hazards.some(s=>s.includes('gauges.minPressure')&&s.includes('new rows')&&s.includes('--to 1 --check --bridge')));
+    assert.match((await g.run(['deploy',g.deployment])).stderr,/rollback foresight.*gauges.minPressure/);
+    const h=await g.serve();assert.equal((await h.post('/m/add',{},'rel5-new-gauge')).state,'sent');
+    const rollback=await g.invoke(['rollback',g.deployment,'--to','1','--check']);assert.equal(rollback.code,1);assert.match(rollback.stderr,/minPressure/);
+    await h.host.stop();active.delete(h.host);
+
+    const c=fixture('contracts');
+    // Execute both Quarry blocks from the installed guide, without source substitutions.
+    const guide=(await c.run(['guide','deploy','--section','Keep v1 clients working'])).stdout;
+    const blocks=[...guide.matchAll(/```quarry\n([\s\S]*?)```/g)].map(m=>m[1]);assert.equal(blocks.length,2,guide);
+    c.source(blocks[0]);await c.run(['deploy',c.deployment]);
+    const oldHost=await c.serve();const frozenArgs={name:'Ada',obsolete:'old input'};
+    assert.deepEqual((await oldHost.post('/q/greet',frozenArgs)).data,{message:'Ada!'});
+    c.source(blocks[1]);
+    const safe=JSON.parse((await c.run(['deploy',c.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout);assert.deepEqual(safe.clientCompatibility,[]);
+    await c.run(['deploy',c.deployment]);
+    assert.deepEqual((await oldHost.post('/q/greet',frozenArgs)).data,{message:'Ada!'});
+    assert.deepEqual((await oldHost.post('/q/greeting',{name:'Ada'})).data,{message:'Ada!'});
+    await oldHost.host.stop();active.delete(oldHost.host);
+    const unsafe=blocks[1].replace("suffix: text <=1 default '!'","suffix: text <=1").replace(', obsolete: text <=40 deprecated','');
+    c.source(unsafe);
+    const diff=JSON.parse((await c.run(['deploy',c.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout).clientCompatibility.join('\n');
+    assert.match(diff,/greet.*argument suffix retyped or narrowed/);assert.match(diff,/greet.*removed argument obsolete/);
+    mkdirSync(join(c.cwd,'versions/v1'),{recursive:true});writeFileSync(join(c.cwd,'versions/v1/schema.q'),blocks[0]);
+    writeFileSync(join(c.cwd,'snapback/client-compatibility.json'),JSON.stringify({baseline:'versions/v1',since_release:2}));
+    const baseline=await c.run(['check']);assert.match(baseline.stderr,/old-client compatibility \(baseline .*new required argument suffix/);
+    const generated=readFileSync(join(c.cwd,'snapback/generated/api.ts'),'utf8');assert.match(generated,/suffix: string/);assert.doesNotMatch(generated,/suffix\?:/);
+    const coldRollback=await c.run(['rollback',c.deployment,'--to','1']);
+    assert.match(coldRollback.stderr,/old-client compatibility.*release 2.*greeting.*removed operation/);
+    const hotRollback=await c.serve();
+    await c.run(['rollback',c.deployment,'--to','2']);
+    const hotWarning=await c.run(['rollback',c.deployment,'--to','1']);
+    assert.match(hotWarning.stderr,/old-client compatibility.*release 2.*greeting.*removed operation/);
+    await hotRollback.host.stop();active.delete(hotRollback.host);
+    const d=fixture('projected-domains');
+    const rows=`use identity guests
+table items:
+  n: int 0..10
+  label: text 1..20
+  public 'result domain proof'
+query list():
+  return items first 10 by .id {n, label}
+`;
+    d.source(rows);await d.run(['deploy',d.deployment]);
+    d.source(rows.replace('int 0..10','int 0..20').replace('text 1..20','text 0..20'));
+    const domains=JSON.parse((await d.run(['deploy',d.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout).clientCompatibility.join('\n');
+    assert.match(domains,/RESULT REVIEW.*items.label/);assert.match(domains,/RESULT REVIEW.*items.n/);
+    return {status:'passed',reactivation:['cold','hot','same-id','dormant-values','unchanged'],refusals:['adoption','post-validation-pointer','no-candidate-left'],rollbackForesight:'dropped-required-column-and-later-writes',oldClients:'installed-guide-signature-diff-baseline-projected-domains'};
+  } finally {
+    try {for(const host of active)await host.stop();} finally {rmSync(root,{recursive:true,force:true});}
+  }
+}

//! Column and parameter types, including precise unsupported-default repairs.
use super::Parser;
use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Fallible, Site, syntax, syntax_with}, lexer::Kind, print};
impl Parser {
    pub(super) fn parse_args(&mut self) -> Fallible<Vec<ArgDecl>> {
        self.take("(")?;
        let mut args = Vec::new();
        while !self.at(")") {
            let name = self.peek().clone();
            let binding = self.local_name()?;
            self.take(":")?;
            let type_site = self.peek().site.clone();
            let type_ = self.parse_type(false).map_err(|mut d| {
                if d.mechanical && d.message.starts_with("once is a column modifier") {
                    // Nested element types need a whole-list rewrite, not an argument fragment.
                    if d.site == type_site { d.site = name.site.clone(); d.rewrite = format!("{binding}: {}", d.rewrite); }
                    else { d.mechanical = false; }
                }
                d
            })?;
            let default = if self.maybe("default") { Some(self.expression(0, &[",", ")", "deprecated"])? ) } else { None };
            let deprecated = self.maybe("deprecated");
            if self.at("=") { return Err(syntax(&self.peek().site,"argument defaults use `default <literal>`")); }
            args.push(ArgDecl { name: binding, type_, default, deprecated, site: name.site });
            if !self.maybe(",") {
                break;
            }
        }
        self.take(")")?;
        Ok(args)
    }

    pub(super) fn parse_type(&mut self, column: bool) -> Fallible<ScalarType> {
        let site = self.peek().site.clone();
        let mut kind;
        if self.maybe("[") {
            let item = self.parse_type(column)?;
            self.take("]")?;
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — list ranges share text's inclusive spelling.
            let bounds_error = |site: &Site| syntax_with(site, "list bounds require <=N or an inclusive a..b range", "write [T] <=N or [T] a..b, for example [int] 1..8").guide("types");
            let number = |parser: &mut Self| -> Fallible<u32> {
                if parser.at("-") {
                    return Err(syntax(&parser.peek().site, "list bounds must be nonnegative integers"));
                }
                if !parser.at_kind(Kind::Number) { return Err(bounds_error(&parser.peek().site)); }
                let token = parser.take_kind(Kind::Number)?;
                if token.value.contains('.') {
                    return Err(syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers; fractional bounds are not allowed"));
                }
                token.value.parse::<u32>().map_err(|_| syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers from 0 through 4294967295"))
            };
            let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
                (0, number(self)?)
            } else {
                let min = number(self)?;
                if !self.maybe(".") || !self.maybe(".") { return Err(bounds_error(&self.peek().site)); }
                (min, number(self)?)
            };
            if min > max {
                return Err(syntax(&site, "list minimum must not exceed its maximum"));
            }
            if item.optional {
                return Err(syntax(&site, "list element types cannot be optional"));
            }
            kind = TypeKind::List { item: Box::new(item), min, max };
        } else {
            let name = self.word()?;
            kind = match name.value.as_str() {
                "text" => {
                    let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
                        (None, self.number()?)
                    } else if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) {
                        let min = self.number()?;
                        self.take(".")?;
                        self.take(".")?;
                        (Some(min), self.number()?)
                    } else {
                        // The default is normalized here; every downstream
                        // validator and resource calculation sees the same bound.
                        (None, 10_000)
                    };
                    let mut format = None;
                    if self.maybe("format") {
                        let value = self.word()?;
                        if !matches!(value.value.as_str(), "handle" | "url" | "email") {
                            let bound = match min {
                                Some(min) => format!("{min}..{max}"),
                                None => format!("<={max}"),
                            };
                            return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("unknown text format {}", value.value), format!("use text {bound} format handle, text {bound} format url, or text {bound} format email")));
                        }
                        format = Some(value.value);
                    }
                    TypeKind::Text { min, max, format }
                }
                "decimal" => TypeKind::Decimal { scale: self.scale("decimal 12")? },
                "money" => {
                    let currency = self.word()?;
                    if currency.value.len() != 3 || !currency.value.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_uppercase()) {
                        return Err(syntax_with(&currency.site, "money currency must be exactly three uppercase letters", "use money USD or money USD 2"));
                    }
                    let scale = if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) { self.scale(&format!("money {} 12", currency.value))? } else { 2 };
                    TypeKind::Money { currency: currency.value, scale }
                }
                "json" => {
                    self.take("<=")?;
                    TypeKind::Json { max: self.number()? }
                }
                "bytes" => {
                    self.take("<=")?;
                    TypeKind::Bytes { max: self.number()? }
                }
                "image" => {
                    if !self.maybe("<=") { return Err(syntax_with(&site, "image needs a maximum upload size in bytes", "write `image <=5MB` (5,000,000 bytes), optionally followed by once or ?").guide("types")); }
                    TypeKind::Image { max: self.media_size()?, once: false }
                }
                "video" => {
                    self.take("<=")?;
                    TypeKind::Video { max: self.media_size()? }
                }
                // `file` is a type only before `<=`: a table named `file` stays a reference.
                // @ref LLP 3000.038#1 — the allow-list names only types the server can verify against the bytes.
                "file" if self.at("<=") => {
                    self.take("<=")?;
                    let max = self.number()?;
                    let mut types = Vec::new();
                    if self.maybe("(") {
                        while !self.at(")") {
                            let value = self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?;
                            if let Some(why) = snapback4_core::assets::file_type_refusal(&value.value) {
                                return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, why, format!("file <={max} ('application/pdf', 'text/plain')")).guide("assets"));
                            }
                            if types.contains(&value.value) {
                                return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("'{}' is listed twice", value.value), "list each media type once"));
                            }
                            types.push(value.value);
                            if !self.maybe(",") { break; }
                        }
                        self.take(")")?;
                        if types.is_empty() {
                            return Err(syntax_with(&site, "a file allow-list needs at least one media type", format!("file <={max} ('application/pdf'), or file <={max} for every verifiable type")).guide("assets"));
                        }
                    }
                    TypeKind::File { max, types }
                }
                "cursor" => TypeKind::Cursor,
                "id" => TypeKind::Id,
                "principal" => TypeKind::Principal,
                "int" if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) || matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "<=" | ">=" | "<" | ">" | "-" | ".") => {
                    let (min, max) = self.int_range(&name.site)?;

//! Immutable release custody: digest the required tree and flush it before DB selection.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5-hot-activation-and-ownership — source durability precedes the database's release pointer.
use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
use std::{
    fs,
    io::{Read, Write},
    path::{Path, PathBuf},
};

fn files(root: &Path, path: &Path, out: &mut Vec<PathBuf>) -> Result<(), String> {
    let mut entries = fs::read_dir(path)
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
        .map(|e| e.map(|e| e.path()).map_err(|e| e.to_string()))
        .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
    entries.sort();
    for path in entries {
        let relative = path.strip_prefix(root).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        if relative
            .components()
            .next()
            .is_some_and(|c| c.as_os_str() == ".snapback4")
        {
            continue;
        }
        let m = fs::symlink_metadata(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        if m.is_dir() {
            files(root, &path, out)?;
        } else if m.is_file() {
            out.push(path);
        } else {
            return Err(format!(
                "immutable release artifact must be a real directory or regular file: {}",
                path.display()
            ));
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
pub(crate) fn digest(root: &Path) -> Result<String, String> {
    let mut paths = Vec::new();
    files(root, root, &mut paths)?;
    let mut hash = Sha256::new();
    for path in paths {
        let name = path.strip_prefix(root).unwrap().to_string_lossy();
        // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000.000#4-cli-surface-contracts — metadata does not change executable release identity.
        if name == ".pending-adoption" || name == "provenance.json" {continue;}
        hash.update((name.len() as u64).to_le_bytes());
        hash.update(name.as_bytes());
        #[cfg(unix)]
        use std::os::unix::fs::OpenOptionsExt;
        let mut options = fs::OpenOptions::new();
        options.read(true);
        #[cfg(unix)]
        options.custom_flags(libc::O_NOFOLLOW);
        let mut file = options.open(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        hash.update(
            file.metadata()
                .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
                .len()
                .to_le_bytes(),
        );
        let mut buffer = [0u8; 16384];
        loop {
            let n = file.read(&mut buffer).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            if n == 0 {
                break;
            }
            hash.update(&buffer[..n]);
        }
    }
    Ok(format!("{:x}", hash.finalize()))
}
pub(crate) fn durable(root: &Path) -> Result<(), String> {
    let mut paths = Vec::new();
    files(root, root, &mut paths)?;
    for path in paths {
        fs::File::open(path)
            .and_then(|f| f.sync_all())
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    }
    fn dirs(path: &Path) -> Result<(), String> {
        for e in fs::read_dir(path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())? {
            let e = e.map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            if e.file_type().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?.is_dir() {
                dirs(&e.path())?;
            }
        }
        fs::File::open(path)
            .and_then(|f| f.sync_all())
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())
    }
    dirs(root)
}

pub(crate) fn link_store(root: &Path) -> Result<(), String> {
    let project = crate::project::Project { root: root.into() };
    snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&project.data_dir()).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let path = project.store_path();
    #[cfg(unix)]
    {
        let target = Path::new("../../../store.sqlite");
        match fs::symlink_metadata(&path) {
            Ok(m) if m.file_type().is_symlink() => {
                if fs::read_link(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())? != target {
                    fs::remove_file(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
                    std::os::unix::fs::symlink(target, &path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
                }
            }
            Ok(_) => {
                return Err(format!(
                    "release store path must be a symbolic link: {}",
                    path.display()
                ));
            }
            Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
                std::os::unix::fs::symlink(target, &path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
            }
            Err(e) => return Err(e.to_string()),
        }
        Ok(())
    }
    #[cfg(not(unix))]
    Err("serving shared releases requires Unix symbolic links".into())
}

pub(crate) struct Pointer {
    staged: PathBuf,
    dir: PathBuf,
    release: u64,
    previous: Option<u64>,
    rollback: bool,
    ledger: fs::File,
}
impl Pointer {
    pub(crate) fn prepare(
        dir: &Path,
        release: u64,
        previous: Option<u64>,
        rollback: bool,
    ) -> Result<Self, String> {
        let pointer = dir.join("current");
        if pointer.exists() && !pointer.is_file() {
            return Err("current must be a regular release pointer file".into());
        }
        let staged = dir.join(format!(".current-{}-{release}", std::process::id()));
        let file = snapback4_core::private_fs::file(&staged, true).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        let mut prepared = Self {
            staged,
            dir: dir.into(),
            release,
            previous,
            rollback,
            ledger: file,
        };
        writeln!(prepared.ledger, "{release}")
            .and_then(|_| prepared.ledger.sync_all())
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        prepared.ledger = fs::OpenOptions::new()
            .create(true)
            .append(true)
            .open(dir.join("releases.jsonl"))
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        writeln!(prepared.ledger,"{}",serde_json::json!({"at":snapback4_core::now_ms(),"release":release,"previous":previous,"rollback":rollback,"state":"prepared"})).and_then(|_|prepared.ledger.sync_all()).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        Ok(prepared)
    }
    pub(crate) fn finish(&mut self, generation: u64) -> Result<(), String> {
        fs::rename(&self.staged,self.dir.join("current")).and_then(|_|fs::File::open(&self.dir)?.sync_all()).map_err(|e|format!("release {} committed in database, but current copy failed: {e}; inspect `snapback4 releases {}`",self.release,self.dir.display()))?;
        writeln!(self.ledger,"{}",serde_json::json!({"at":snapback4_core::now_ms(),"release":self.release,"previous":self.previous,"rollback":self.rollback,"generation":generation,"state":"activated"})).and_then(|_|self.ledger.sync_all()).map_err(|e|format!("release committed, but ledger completion failed: {e}"))
    }
}
impl Drop for Pointer {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if self.staged.exists() {
            let _ = fs::remove_file(&self.staged);
        }
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#16-release-reactivation-and-rollback-foresight-rel5 — deletion requires a definitive non-commit, never a lost reply.
pub(crate) struct Candidate { dir: PathBuf, release: u64, pub committed: bool, pub pending: bool }
impl Candidate {
    pub(crate) fn new(dir: &Path, release: u64) -> Self { Self {dir:dir.into(),release,committed:false,pending:false} }
}
impl Drop for Candidate {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if self.committed { return; }
        if self.pending {eprintln!("release {} result remains unknown; retained source while the server may still commit; inspect releases",self.release);return;}
        let probe=(|| -> Result<bool,String> {
            let store=snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(&self.dir.join("store.sqlite")).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            let history:bool=store.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE type='table' AND name='_release_events')",[],|r|r.get(0)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            if !history {return Ok(false);}
            store.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _release_events WHERE release=?1 OR previous=?1)",[self.release as i64],|r|r.get(0)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())
        })();
        match probe {
            Ok(false)=>if let Err(e)=fs::remove_dir_all(self.dir.join("releases").join(self.release.to_string())) {eprintln!("uncommitted release {} cleanup failed: {e}",self.release);},
            Ok(true)=>{},
            Err(e)=>eprintln!("release {} outcome unknown; retained source for recovery; inspect releases: {e}",self.release),
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod candidate_tests {
    use super::*;
    #[test]
    fn cleanup_removes_only_definitively_uncommitted_candidates() {
        let root=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-candidate-custody-{}-{}",std::process::id(),snapback4_core::now_ms()));
        fs::create_dir_all(root.join("releases")).unwrap();
        struct Cleanup(PathBuf);
        impl Drop for Cleanup {fn drop(&mut self){let _=fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);}}
        let _cleanup=Cleanup(root.clone());
        let store=snapback4_core::Store::open(&root.join("store.sqlite")).unwrap();
        store.conn().execute_batch("CREATE TABLE _release_events(release INTEGER,previous INTEGER)").unwrap();
        for n in 1..=3 {fs::create_dir(root.join("releases").join(n.to_string())).unwrap();}
        drop(Candidate::new(&root,1));
        assert!(!root.join("releases/1").exists());
        // A server may commit after this history read. No observed row is not a completed refusal.
        let mut flight=Candidate::new(&root,2);flight.pending=true;drop(flight);
        assert!(root.join("releases/2").is_dir());
        store.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _release_events VALUES(3,NULL)",[]).unwrap();
        drop(Candidate::new(&root,3));
        assert!(root.join("releases/3").is_dir());
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#16-release-reactivation-and-rollback-foresight-rel5 — run the same transitions with development and installed binaries.
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { mkdirSync, writeFileSync, readFileSync, readdirSync, mkdtempSync, rmSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join } from 'node:path';

export async function releaseTransitions({ cli, startServe }, parent) {
  const root=mkdtempSync(join(parent,'release-transitions-'));
  const active=new Set();
  try {
    const fixture=name=> {
      const cwd=join(root,name);mkdirSync(join(cwd,'snapback'),{recursive:true});
      const deployment=join(cwd,'deployment'),schema=join(cwd,'snapback/schema.q');
      const invoke=args=>cli(args,{cwd,timeoutMs:60_000});
      const run=async args=>{const r=await invoke(args);assert.equal(r.code,0,r.stdout+r.stderr);return r;};
      const status=async()=>JSON.parse((await run(['releases',deployment,'--json'])).stdout);
      const releases=()=>readdirSync(join(deployment,'releases')).sort();
      const source=text=>writeFileSync(schema,text);
      const serve=async()=> {

        const signed=await (await fetch(host.url+'/auth/guest',{method:'POST',body:'{}'})).json();
        assert.ok(signed.session?.token,JSON.stringify(signed));
        const headers={'content-type':'application/json',authorization:'Bearer '+signed.session.token};
        const post=async(path,args={},id)=> {
          const r=await fetch(host.url+path,{method:'POST',headers,body:JSON.stringify({args,...(id?{id}:{})}),signal:AbortSignal.timeout(30_000)});
          return r.json();
        };
        return {host,post};
      };
      return {cwd,deployment,schema,invoke,run,status,releases,source,serve};
    };
    const f=fixture('reactivation');
    const v1=`use identity guests
table items:
  body: text <=40
  public 'release proof'
  insert <- allow
  update <- allow
mutation add(body: text <=40):
  return insert items {body}
query list():
  return items first 20 by .id
`;
    const v2=v1.replace('  body: text','  extra: text <=40 default \'default\'\n  body: text')+`mutation mark(id: items):
  return update items[id] {extra: 'preserved'}
`;
    f.source(v1);await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment]);
    f.source(v2);await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment]);await f.run(['rollback',f.deployment,'--to','1']);
    const before=await f.status();
    const check=JSON.parse((await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout);
    assert.equal(check.plan.release,2);assert.deepEqual(await f.status(),before);assert.deepEqual(f.releases(),['1','2']);
    assert.equal(JSON.parse((await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment,'--json'])).stdout).release,2);
    const {host,post}=await f.serve();
    const added=await post('/m/add',{body:'kept'},'rel5-add');assert.equal(added.state,'sent',JSON.stringify(added));
    const id=added.result.id;
    assert.equal((await post('/m/mark',{id},'rel5-mark')).state,'sent');
    await f.run(['rollback',f.deployment,'--to','1','--restore-dormant']);
    await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment]);
    assert.equal((await f.status()).release,2);assert.equal((await post('/q/list')).data[0].extra,'preserved');
    const unchanged=await f.status();assert.equal(JSON.parse((await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment,'--json'])).stdout).unchanged,true);
    assert.deepEqual(await f.status(),unchanged);
    f.source(v2.replace('  body: text','  required: int\n  body: text').replace('insert items {body}','insert items {body, required: 1}'));
    const failed=await f.invoke(['deploy',f.deployment]);assert.equal(failed.code,1);assert.match(failed.stderr,/E_ADOPT/);
    assert.deepEqual(f.releases(),['1','2']);assert.deepEqual(await f.status(),unchanged);
    assert.equal((await post('/q/list')).data[0].extra,'preserved');
    await host.stop();active.delete(host);
    // Failure after validation, before database commit, also cleans its numeric source.
    f.source(v2.replace("return items first 20", "return items first 19"));
    rmSync(join(f.deployment,'current'));mkdirSync(join(f.deployment,'current'));
    const pointerFailure=await f.invoke(['deploy',f.deployment]);assert.equal(pointerFailure.code,1);assert.match(pointerFailure.stderr,/current must be a regular/);
    assert.deepEqual(f.releases(),['1','2']);rmSync(join(f.deployment,'current'),{recursive:true});writeFileSync(join(f.deployment,'current'),'2\n');

`;
    const next=prior.replace('  minPressure: int 0..2000\n','  lowPressure: int 0..2000 default 0\n').replace('{minPressure: 4}','{lowPressure: 4}');

    const foresight=JSON.parse((await g.run(['deploy',g.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout);
    assert.ok(foresight.hazards.some(s=>s.includes('gauges.minPressure')&&s.includes('new rows')&&s.includes('--to 1 --check --bridge')));

    assert.deepEqual((await oldHost.post('/q/greet',frozenArgs)).data,{message:'Ada!'});
    assert.deepEqual((await oldHost.post('/q/greeting',{name:'Ada'})).data,{message:'Ada!'});
    await oldHost.host.stop();active.delete(oldHost.host);
    const unsafe=blocks[1].replace("suffix: text <=1 default '!'","suffix: text <=1").replace(', obsolete: text <=40 deprecated','');
    c.source(unsafe);
    const diff=JSON.parse((await c.run(['deploy',c.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout).clientCompatibility.join('\n');
    assert.match(diff,/greet.*argument suffix retyped or narrowed/);assert.match(diff,/greet.*removed argument obsolete/);
    mkdirSync(join(c.cwd,'versions/v1'),{recursive:true});writeFileSync(join(c.cwd,'versions/v1/schema.q'),blocks[0]);
    writeFileSync(join(c.cwd,'snapback/client-compatibility.json'),JSON.stringify({baseline:'versions/v1',since_release:2}));
    const baseline=await c.run(['check']);assert.match(baseline.stderr,/old-client compatibility \(baseline .*new required argument suffix/);
    const generated=readFileSync(join(c.cwd,'snapback/generated/api.ts'),'utf8');assert.match(generated,/suffix: string/);assert.doesNotMatch(generated,/suffix\?:/);
    const coldRollback=await c.run(['rollback',c.deployment,'--to','1']);
    assert.match(coldRollback.stderr,/old-client compatibility.*release 2.*greeting.*removed operation/);
    const hotRollback=await c.serve();
    await c.run(['rollback',c.deployment,'--to','2']);
    const hotWarning=await c.run(['rollback',c.deployment,'--to','1']);
    assert.match(hotWarning.stderr,/old-client compatibility.*release 2.*greeting.*removed operation/);
    await hotRollback.host.stop();active.delete(hotRollback.host);
    const d=fixture('projected-domains');
    const rows=`use identity guests
table items:
  n: int 0..10
  label: text 1..20
  public 'result domain proof'
query list():
  return items first 10 by .id {n, label}
`;
    d.source(rows);await d.run(['deploy',d.deployment]);
    d.source(rows.replace('int 0..10','int 0..20').replace('text 1..20','text 0..20'));
    const domains=JSON.parse((await d.run(['deploy',d.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout).clientCompatibility.join('\n');
    assert.match(domains,/RESULT REVIEW.*items.label/);assert.match(domains,/RESULT REVIEW.*items.n/);
    return {status:'passed',reactivation:['cold','hot','same-id','dormant-values','unchanged'],refusals:['adoption','post-validation-pointer','no-candidate-left'],rollbackForesight:'dropped-required-column-and-later-writes',oldClients:'installed-guide-signature-diff-baseline-projected-domains'};
  } finally {
    try {for(const host of active)await host.stop();} finally {rmSync(root,{recursive:true,force:true});}
  }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ActionDecl {

}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ScheduleDecl {
    pub name: String,
    pub every_ms: u64,
    /// The offset of the period boundary within the period (`at 08:00`).
    pub offset_ms: u64,
    pub target: String,
    pub service: Option<String>,
    /// `every N months` / `every N years` (12N): calendar periods; `every_ms` is 0.
    pub months: u32,
    pub site: Site,
}

pub struct EffectDecl {
    pub name: String,
    pub module: String,
    pub work: WorkDecl,
    pub site: Site,
}
